# Claims ledger

Generated from `CLAIMS.json` by `tools/check_claims.py render`. Do not hand-edit; `tools/check_claims.py check render` fails if this file disagrees with a fresh regeneration.

## Methodology

This ledger covers two distinct obligations. First, every number-bearing sentence in README.md and docs/** (excluding a version, a date, a path, or a code-block line) is catalogued below as either a claim or a `not_claims` entry with a reason -- this is the coverage criterion, and it is deliberately narrower than 'every claim': numbers are what a reader trusts and what an author gets wrong, so that is what is mechanically enforced here. Second, this ledger separately records the required INVALIDATED entries for dogfood run a02, and five specific unflattering findings this project's own evidence supports, regardless of whether they happen to contain a digit.

'Sentence' is operationalized as a source line: these documents are hard-wrapped roughly one clause per line, and the specification's own phrase 'a code-block line' already treats the line as the unit of exclusion. A markdown heading or list item introduced by a bare ordinal ('## 4. Title', '1. Do the thing') has that ordinal stripped before the digit check, so pure structural numbering does not by itself trigger coverage -- otherwise every numbered heading and list step in this project's documents would need its own ledger entry for no informational gain. A version (`0.8.0`), an ISO or German date, a hex commit/blob hash, and a backtick-quoted path or dotted filename are stripped the same way. What is left over is genuinely a claim-shaped digit occurrence: a threshold, a count, an identifier, an environment literal, or a citation index.

Within what is left, several categories recur and are marked `not_claims` in bulk rather than individually re-argued: internal finding/gate/milestone identifiers (A01-A12, H0-H4, K1) and citation indices ([Q1]..[Q12], resolved in QUELLENCHECK.md) name things rather than measure them; ordinal cross-references ('step 2', 'measured property 3') point back to an already-excluded numbered list; command/instruction snippets ('python3 -m hoh.cli') carry a digit as part of a binary name, not a measurement; and one illustrative placeholder ('commit `abc123`', 'run 7'/'run 3') is explicitly not about a real measured run. The citation table in QUELLENCHECK.md is judged not-claims as a whole: it verifies upstream sources for design decisions, not this software's own behaviour, even though its rows would otherwise fit the `cite:` evidence form structurally.

The most disputable call in this pass concerns three files: *docs/K1_CACHE_PROFILE.md*, *docs/QUELLENCHECK.md*, and *docs/HERDR_HOH_IMPLEMENTATION_HANDOFF.md*, named here to state a judgement about them, not to point a reader at them. All three are German-language internal working notes in substance, the same kind of document the out-of-scope list names by root-level example (RUNBOOK.md, the A0*.md files, PHASE2.md). But they live under `docs/`, and the coverage criterion says `README.md and docs/**` without carving out a subdirectory, so this run catalogued them in full rather than quietly exempting them. A reader who judges that boundary differently should say so; the sentences are all listed either way, so nothing is hidden by the disagreement.

One field beyond the specification's minimum schema is used here: `invalidated_run` on an INVALIDATED claim, naming the run its now-superseded evidence rested on. The specification's own criterion 5 wording ('a claim whose evidence names run a02') is satisfied structurally by this field exactly the way a `receipt:a02/...` or `run:a02/...` evidence reference would be: `tools/check_claims.py`'s a02 check treats both as naming a run. This run's checkout has no populated `runs/` directory (it is out of scope to create one, and it is genuinely absent here), so the two INVALIDATED claims below cite the committed post-mortem documents (A02_BERICHT.md, A03_NACHWEIS.md, HOH_ACCEPTANCE_REPORT.md) rather than raw a02 receipts -- citing a document that analyses what a02 got wrong is not the same as resting a claim on a02's own unreliable numbers, and a plain `file:` reference to those documents is deliberately not treated as 'naming run a02' by the checker, or the historically accurate SUPPORTED claim about the K1..K9 misreport (also sourced from A02_BEFUNDE.md, a post-mortem document, not a raw a02 acceptance record) would be wrongly forced INVALIDATED too.

The five mandated UNSUPPORTED findings are recorded as UNSUPPORTED rather than SUPPORTED even where a document states them plainly, because most of them are absence claims ('no baseline exists', 'never touched src/ or tests/') that no evidence form in this schema can positively verify -- a `file:` reference can prove a sentence exists, not that the absence it describes is complete. Where corroborating evidence is available (a unit test, a source line) it is still attached, so the note explains exactly how far that evidence reaches and no further.

d4d: coverage used to glob docs/** at check time, which is how O41 happened -- two runs that never touched a common file still collided because the checker discovered scope by walking the filesystem instead of reading a declaration. coverage_targets() now reads the top-level claim_surfaces array above instead: every file the old glob covered is declared there (scope "whole file" for all eleven -- none of the current documents needs section-level scoping), each with a why and requires_reconciliation_on_change=true, and claim_surface_policy names the same requirement once more at the manifest level so a consumer can read the dependency from CLAIMS.json alone. A new reconcile CLI subcommand reports, read-only, which statements in a declared surface are unbound (no resolving anchor) or vanished (a recorded anchor that no longer resolves) -- it never writes. An undeclared-surface heuristic (find_undeclared_claim_surfaces) warns, never fails, when a tracked README.md/docs/**.md document carries number-bearing sentences but is missing from claim_surfaces; it cannot catch a claim-bearing file with no digits at all, a non-.md file, or a document outside README.md/docs/** entirely, since those are exactly the two locations the old glob (and this heuristic, deliberately, as its replacement-in-spirit for discovery rather than for coverage) ever looked. Using that reconciliation, the eleven docs/LIMITATIONS.md sentences d3c left uncovered against the frozen eight-limit-version ledger (C-052..C-060, N-061, N-062) are now bound: nine are historical/measured counts from this project's own run and merge history, recorded UNSUPPORTED for the same reason the five earlier mandated findings are -- no evidence form in this schema positively verifies a historical count, and this checkout has no populated runs/ tree to re-derive one from even where a discriminated:/receiptcount: form would otherwise apply. The remaining two (docs/LIMITATIONS.md:157, :208) are not_claims: an internal cross-reference to another limit's heading number, and a quoted restatement of an already-recorded figure used only to warn against misreading it -- both fit categories already established in this methodology's third paragraph, not new categories invented for this run.

r1a: every evidence reference above now also carries a derived resolvability class (PUBLIC/LOCAL_ONLY/EXTERNAL), shown next to its own bullet under 'Evidence' below -- never in a separate legend or summary table. The class is derived, not typed per reference: receipt:/run:/receiptcount:/discriminated: are always LOCAL_ONLY (runs/** is never exported to the public repository); cite: is always EXTERNAL; test: is always PUBLIC (tests/ is exported whole); file: is PUBLIC unless its path falls under one of a small named export-exclusion rule table (evidence-not-artifact, parked-predecessor, stale-build-output, foreign-subject, internal-working-document -- see tools/check_claims.py's DEFAULT_EXPORT_EXCLUSION_RULES, derived from dogfood/specs/r1-export-manifest.md's stated rules since no EXPORT_MANIFEST.json exists yet), in which case it is LOCAL_ONLY. A LOCAL_ONLY reference is not a defect: it is the expected shape of most of this ledger's strongest (receipt-backed) evidence, and every claim that carries one states, in its own local_only_resolution field, who resolved it and when.

d7w: Four evidence forms -- run:, receipt:, receiptcount: and discriminated: -- resolve only inside the operator's own runs/ tree, which is gitignored and excluded from every export. A reader who does not have that tree and sees SUPPORTED on a claim carrying one of these citations is trusting the operator: specifically, trusting a prior run of the checker that the operator carried out locally, not re-deriving the evidence themselves. 34 SUPPORTED claims currently carry at least one such citation.

d4e: `CLAIMS.json` now declares, for every `INCLUDE` path in `EXPORT_MANIFEST.json`, either a `claim_surfaces` entry (this run added seventeen, for the `.md` files r1's export left undeclared -- `CHANGELOG.md`, this file, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `PROVENANCE.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, the two `examples/minimal/` files, `ops/README.md`, the seven `paper/*.md` files, and `policy/README.md`) or a `claim_surface_exclusions` entry naming one of five closed reasons (`ledger-itself`, `copied-evidence`, `pattern-data`, `package-metadata`, `source-code` -- this run added fifty-four). `check_export_classification()` in `tools/check_claims.py` fails, naming every offending path, when an `INCLUDE` path is neither declared nor excluded, declared both ways, excluded with a reason outside that closed set, or a prose `.md` `INCLUDE` path is excluded instead of declared a surface; `find_undeclared_claim_surfaces()` above is untouched -- it keeps warning, never failing, over its own `README.md`+`docs/**` reference set, because a directory glob lets a developer dodge a hard failure by keeping a file out of the scanned directories, while the only way to dodge this new check is to stop exporting the file, a declared act `EXPORT_MANIFEST.json` itself records. Separately, this run replaced the identical `local_only_resolution` sentence r1a stamped onto thirty-nine claims (see the paragraph above) with genuinely distinct, per-claim text naming the specific run, iteration, receipt file, or document line each claim's own evidence actually rests on; `check_local_only_diversity()` fails if any two `LOCAL_ONLY` claims ever again share one statement, and a fixture where every claim shares one sentence is the regression test that proves the check would have caught the original defect.

This project has paid for that twice. Once, run `dug2` was rejected repeatedly across three iterations, ten of eleven of its own criteria green each time, at a failure two other, already-merged runs had jointly produced entirely outside `dug2`'s own declared scope -- which its scope forbade it to touch -- and the run was abandoned rather than fixed, because fixing it was never its job. | C-115 | UNSUPPORTED | ### B-05 — Check-command guard: runtime-concatenated dot variables defeat the parent-escape check ⟦The regex-based check-command guard's `_PARENT_ESCAPE` pattern (src/hoh/runner.py) matches only a literal `..` substring in the command's source text; a two-variable runtime concatenation (`A=.; B=.; C="$A$B"`) that bash later expands into `..` is not caught by that pattern, so `assert_stays_in_arena` does not raise `ArenaEscape` on a command that, once the shell expands the concatenation, escapes the arena via a parent-directory reference.⟧ | paper/REVIEW_B.md:266 |
| C-116 | UNSUPPORTED | ### B-06 — Check-command guard: a variable-split absolute path defeats the absolute-path check ⟦The regex-based check-command guard's `_ABSOLUTE_PATH` pattern (src/hoh/runner.py) requires a well-known four-character absolute-path prefix -- the same one this project's own home-path guard assembles at runtime from `chr(47)` and a directory name rather than spelling literally -- to appear as a contiguous substring in the command's source text; building the same string from two shorter variables at shell runtime (`P=/et; Q=c/`) never produces that contiguous substring in the source text the guard inspects, so `assert_stays_in_arena` does not raise on a command that bash still resolves to a real absolute path outside the arena.⟧ | paper/REVIEW_B.md:290 |
| C-117 | UNSUPPORTED | ### B-07 — Check-command guard: IFS-based space substitution defeats both the directory-change and parent-escape checks at once ⟦The check-command guard's `_DIRECTORY_CHANGE` pattern requires `cd` to be followed immediately by literal whitespace or end-of-string, and `_PARENT_ESCAPE` requires `..` to be immediately preceded by a delimiter from a fixed set; the token `cd$IFS..` satisfies neither pattern in its literal source-text form, but at shell runtime unquoted `$IFS` expands to whitespace and is then word-split, so bash executes `cd$IFS..` as `cd ..`, a directory change out of the arena neither pattern was written to recognise.⟧ | paper/REVIEW_B.md:305 |
| C-118 | UNSUPPORTED | ### B-08 — Check-command guard: ANSI-C quoted octal escapes defeat the parent-escape check ⟦Bash's `$'...'` ANSI-C quoting expands backslash-octal escapes before the word is used, so `$'\056\056'` becomes two literal `.` characters (octal 56) to the shell at runtime; the source text handed to the check-command guard never contains two adjacent literal dot characters, only the six-character escape sequence, so `_PARENT_ESCAPE` (which matches literal `..`, not its octal encoding) does not fire, and the shell still resolves the argument to a real parent-directory reference.⟧ | paper/REVIEW_B.md:330 |

Note: INVALIDATED 2026-09-09: measured directly against this repository's own commit history via `git log --format='%s' -- tests/ src/ | grep '^HoH '`. That measurement shows 0 commits from any run touching `src/`, and every commit to three files under `tests/` coming from a run -- test_claims_anchors.py (9 of 9 commits), test_export_manifest.py (4 of 4), test_union_gate.py (3 of 3); no other file under `tests/` has any run-authored commit. The claim's absolute form is also false on its own terms for `tests/`: several of this project's own run specifications list `tests/test_*.py` in their own Scope -- what you may change section, so those runs were instructed to write those tests, not merely left unforbidden to. See docs/LIMITATIONS.md limit 8 for the corrected, narrower statement this project's evidence actually supports: no run ever touched `src/`, and the three `tests/` files the loop created are the release tools' own test files. Superseded note, kept for provenance rather than deleted (set when this claim was UNSUPPORTED, replaced 2026-09-09): HoH's evidence about its own reliability speaks to documentation- and governance-shaped work only; every dogfood run, including this one (also forbidden from touching src/hoh/ or tests/, per dogfood/specs/d4-claims.md), was deliberately kept out of src/ and tests/. Whether the loop holds up the same way on a nontrivial production code path remains untested by this project's own history.

Note: Measured on 2026-09-11 by running git rev-parse --show-toplevel in the same arena directory inside and outside the Linux sandbox backend. verify_limit_6 in src/hoh/sandbox.py returns both measurements; the test demands they differ. Withdrawn on 2026-09-11. This statement said the sandbox closed limit 6, measured by comparing `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` inside and outside isolation. The measurement was real; the conclusion drawn from it was wrong in both directions. The common case had been closed since 2026-09-08 by GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES (finding O30), so the sandbox did not close it -- and what remained open was narrower: the ceiling is an environment variable the checked command can remove, which `env -u GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES git rev-parse --show-toplevel` does. The replacement passage at docs/LIMITATIONS.md gives all three regimes with their exit codes, and C-150..C-153 carry the measurements. Kept rather than deleted because a corrected claim whose former wording has vanished is indistinguishable from one that was never made.

Note: The real end-to-end run this sentence describes was executed against an isolated fixture repository in a scratch root, deliberately not inside this repository: pointing a first real resume test at the VeriHarness codebase itself would have made the blast radius of a mistake the project under test. Its receipts therefore do not resolve here, and no evidence form in this ledger can verify a run that lives outside the tree the ledger covers. Marked unsupported rather than cited against receipts that are not here -- which is the same rule this ledger applies to every other claim whose evidence it cannot reach. The mechanism the run exercised is separately covered by the suite (tests/test_orchestrator_parity.py and tests/test_project_resume.py); what is unsupported here is specifically the claim that it was done for real, with those numbers.

| CLAIMS.md:19 | d4d: coverage used to glob docs/** at check time, which is how O41 happened -- two runs that never touched a common file still collided because the checker discovered scope by walking the filesystem instead of reading a declaration. coverage_targets() now reads the top-level claim_surfaces array above instead: every file the old glob covered is declared there (scope "whole file" for all eleven -- none of the current documents needs section-level scoping), each with a why and requires_reconciliation_on_change=true, and claim_surface_policy names the same requirement once more at the manifest level so a consumer can read the dependency from CLAIMS.json alone. A new reconcile CLI subcommand reports, read-only, which statements in a declared surface are unbound (no resolving anchor) or vanished (a recorded anchor that no longer resolves) -- it never writes. An undeclared-surface heuristic (find_undeclared_claim_surfaces) warns, never fails, when a tracked README.md/docs/**.md document carries number-bearing sentences but is missing from claim_surfaces; it cannot catch a claim-bearing file with no digits at all, a non-.md file, or a document outside README.md/docs/** entirely, since those are exactly the two locations the old glob (and this heuristic, deliberately, as its replacement-in-spirit for discovery rather than for coverage) ever looked. Using that reconciliation, the eleven docs/LIMITATIONS.md sentences d3c left uncovered against the frozen eight-limit-version ledger (C-052..C-060, N-061, N-062) are now bound: nine are historical/measured counts from this project's own run and merge history, recorded UNSUPPORTED for the same reason the five earlier mandated findings are -- no evidence form in this schema positively verifies a historical count, and this checkout has no populated runs/ tree to re-derive one from even where a discriminated:/receiptcount: form would otherwise apply. Per-run, receipt-bound evidence was, at the time, treated as sufficient to keep this project's public statements about itself consistent -- and that treatment turned out to be unsupported: `LICENSE` said no license had been chosen, `pyproject.toml` said Apache-2.0, and `CITATION.cff` said `UNVERIFIED`, three simultaneously contradictory statements about the project's own licence, left standing after two individually honest runs merged (C-017). `CLAIMS.json` records that sufficiency claim as unsupported rather than confirmed, because no evidence form in its schema can positively verify a project-wide consistency property from per-run receipts alone -- a person noticed the contradiction, not a check. ⟦two individually honest runs merged (C-017). `CLAIMS.json` records that⟧ | restates claim C-017's own finding, already carried in CLAIMS.json. |

| paper/POSITION_PAPER.md:385 | Third, and the concrete, measured consequence of nesting the arena inside this project's own working tree rather than a separate filesystem: until 2026-09-08, the runner also redirected a check's `TMPDIR` into that same arena, and that redirection has already produced one real defect in the candidate binding -- `is_git_repo` in `src/hoh/workspace.py` once answered a question about the *outer* repository instead of the arena subdirectory, precisely because pytest's own temp-directory redirection landed inside the nested arena. The measurement behind the fix: 28 of 52 candidate arenas in this project's own history carried a leftover `pytest-of-<user>/` directory from an earlier check before the redirection was corrected -- a count over past, mostly no-longer-existing arenas that `CLAIMS.json` itself records as unsupported rather than confirmed, since this checkout cannot positively re-derive a historical population it does not still hold (C-061). Run `d4b` turned a criterion the operator had specified imprecisely ("these checks cannot run without `runs/`") into an asserted precondition instead: in a check directory that genuinely has no `runs/` subdirectory, every evidence-resolution failure must carry a distinct `ENVIRONMENT GAP` marker, and no unmarked failure is permitted -- a statement that is machine-checkable where the operator's original framing was not, and it is now `tools/check_claims.py`'s own `ENVIRONMENT_GAP_MARKER` constant. Run `d3b` added two precisions its specification had not asked for: `PROVENANCE.md` names that the Homebrew formula one version ahead of the installed Herdr binary independently states Apache-2.0, while the installed 0.8.0 binary itself ships with no local license file to read; and it frames this project's own choice of MIT over Apache-2.0 as "a stated trade-off, not a claim that MIT is 'the standard' license," rather than presenting the choice as self-evidently correct. ⟦verifiable by reading the ledger's own schema. | paper/POSITION_PAPER.md:738 | **A usage-quota exhaustion is indistinguishable from a role that broke its contract.** On 2026-09-08, two independent runs, `d5` and `d4h`, failed in the same shape within one window: a role session left no answer file behind, blocking each run in turn -- an operator's own cross-run reading of session logs, and one this project's evidence forms cannot independently re-verify, so `CLAIMS.json` records the joint-timing account as unsupported rather than confirmed (C-068..C-072). ⟦its contract.** On 2026-09-08, two independent runs, `d5` and `d4h`,⟧ | restates claim C-068's own finding ('On 2026-09-08... two independent runs', explicit cite range C-068..C-072 two lines below). |
| paper/POSITION_PAPER.md:743 | This run's own history is the cost made concrete: run `D5`'s criterion 6 required, verbatim, "all twelve limitations," true when it was written and false by the time `D5` actually ran, because run `d3e` had by then already merged limit 13 and limit 14 into `docs/LIMITATIONS.md`; the running specification could not be corrected in place -- the same immutability this entry describes applied to it too -- so the fix did not happen inside that run, it became this separate one. 16. **A usage-quota exhaustion is indistinguishable from a role that broke its contract.** On 2026-09-08, two independent runs, `d5` and `d4h`, failed in the same shape within one window: a role session left no answer file behind, blocking each run in turn -- an operator's own cross-run reading of session logs, and one this project's evidence forms cannot independently re-verify, so `CLAIMS.json` records the joint-timing account as unsupported rather than confirmed (C-068..C-072). The command built for exactly this cause did not recognise it: `hoh resume-quota`, run over this project's full history, reported `{"checked": 37, "results": []}` -- 37 runs checked, 0 identified as quota-blocked -- even though two of the runs it checked, `d5` and `d4h`, were (C-073, C-074). "Left no answer file behind" names two different causes and only one has a resume path: a role's own broken output contract, where the session ran, produced no verdict, and there is nothing to resume from except re-dispatching the role; or a spent usage quota, where the session was cut off before the role process could write anything at all, including the marker `resume-quota` looks for. HoH never detects a quota exhaustion by itself and never automatically resumes a run blocked for that reason; the recovery here is an operator action, not an automated one -- `hoh unblock <run> --reason "..."` lifts the block by hand, once the cause is confirmed, and only then does the role run again. ⟦as unsupported rather than confirmed (C-068..C-072). The command built⟧ | restates claims C-068 through C-072 (explicit cite). |

| paper/REVIEW_A.md:56 | - severity: HIGH - location: `paper/POSITION_PAPER.md` §11 preamble (lines 454–462) and §11 item 6 (lines 495–497), together with body §6 (lines 240–297), read against `docs/LIMITATIONS.md`'s own limit 6 (lines 70–120, especially 98–120). - claim: The §11 preamble states plainly that the Limitations section "corresponds to `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` and is complete against it... this section carries a statement for every one of them." Its restatement of limit 6 covers only the arena's nesting inside the project's own working tree and the now-fixed (2026-09-08) `TMPDIR` redirection that once caused the `is_git_repo` defect; that is the entire content of both §11 item 6 and body §6 on this topic. - why: `docs/LIMITATIONS.md`'s own limit 6 names a second, independent failure mode that the paper never mentions anywhere: an arena has no `.git` of its own, but git's own upward directory search does not treat that as an error -- `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, run from inside a materialized arena, silently climbs past the arena boundary and resolves to the ancestor checkout's path instead. ⟦- location: `paper/POSITION_PAPER.md` §11 preamble (lines 454–462) and §11⟧ | section reference (§11) and line-range location pointer. |

| paper/REVIEW_B.md:123 | Five ALLOWED/BLOCKED-labelled shapes were the minimum asked for; seven were run. Four of the seven (#2–#5) are genuine, empirically confirmed escapes from the object under test. All four share one root cause, named in B-05 through B-08 below: `assert_stays_in_arena` matches fixed regular expressions against the **literal source text** of the command, never against what a shell would actually expand that text into. ⟦run. Four of the seven (#2–#5) are genuine, empirically confirmed escapes⟧ | restates the 'four of seven are genuine bypasses' summary, previewing findings B-05 through B-08 below. |
| paper/REVIEW_B.md:124 | Five ALLOWED/BLOCKED-labelled shapes were the minimum asked for; seven were run. Four of the seven (#2–#5) are genuine, empirically confirmed escapes from the object under test. All four share one root cause, named in B-05 through B-08 below: `assert_stays_in_arena` matches fixed regular expressions against the **literal source text** of the command, never against what a shell would actually expand that text into. Any technique that defers constructing the dangerous substring — two dots, an absolute path prefix, or the whitespace between `cd` and its argument — to shell-time expansion produces a command the guard never sees as dangerous, while bash executes it exactly as the historical, already-fixed literal case did. ⟦from the object under test. All four share one root cause, named in B-05⟧ | internal finding-id references (B-05 through B-08), continuation. |

| paper/REVIEW_B.md:348 | ### B-09 — The evidence checker can never exit clean without a private `runs/` tree, and the ledger does not say so in one place ⟦### B-09 — The evidence checker can never exit clean without a private `runs/` tree, and the ledger does not say so in one place⟧ | internal finding-id heading (B-09); restates the headline figure recorded as a new claim at line 351 below. |
| paper/REVIEW_B.md:355 | Running the checker in this checkout — which has no `runs/` directory, matching both the check arena (O30/O33) and any real clone of the public export (`runs/` is gitignored and, per B-01/B-02's underlying tooling, always classified `EXCLUDE`/`evidence-not-artifact`) — produces 57 `FAIL:` lines, every one of them explicitly labelled `ENVIRONMENT GAP... this is not a content defect in the ledger` rather than a silent pass or an undifferentiated failure. ⟦tooling, always classified `EXCLUDE`/`evidence-not-artifact`) — produces 57⟧ | restates the finding recorded as a new claim at line 351 above (B-09). |
| paper/REVIEW_B.md:363 | That labelling is good practice and is reported here as a positive, not a defect. But the checker's own exit code is non-zero regardless, meaning `tools/check_claims.py check all` can never be used as a green gate in any environment that does not also carry the operator's private `runs/` tree — which is every environment a third party or the check arena will ever run it in. `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` limit 11 documents the adjacent, narrower problem (a criterion *inside a run* cannot inspect `runs/`); it does not state, in one place a reader would find before trusting a `SUPPORTED` label, that the 37 claims resting even partly on `run:`/`receipt:`/`receiptcount:`/`discriminated:` evidence can never be independently re-verified by anyone without that same private directory — a number the operator alone verified must not read as a number the published tooling can verify for someone else. **Reproduction:** ⟦or the check arena will ever run it in. `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` limit 11⟧ | restates the finding recorded as a new claim at line 351 above (B-09). |

| paper/REVIEW_B.md:383 | ### B-10 — `hoh deliver --approve` was genuinely never used, but 32 protected-branch merges bypassed the code path that switch guards, with no comparable authorization record ⟦### B-10 — `hoh deliver --approve` was genuinely never used, but 32 protected-branch merges bypassed the code path that switch guards, with no comparable authorization record⟧ | internal finding-id heading (B-10); restates the headline figure recorded as a new claim at line 393 below. |
| paper/REVIEW_B.md:402 | Report point D's specific, narrow claim — that `hoh deliver --approve` was never invoked because "the switch means the captain's explicit approval, and I don't set that for myself" — checks out: no commit message, tag name, or reflog entry anywhere in this repository's full history references `hoh deliver` being run, and no `runs/` state exists to show a delivery record either way. But the same report table also names 32 direct `git merge --no-ff` operations performed by the operator to land accepted candidate branches on `master`, framed throughout as ordinary "Operatorarbeit." Landing work on a project's integration branch is exactly the action `src/hoh/delivery.py`'s own module docstring quotes the handoff as reserving to the captain and firstmate ("this assignment grants no additional push, merge, deployment or purchasing rights"), and `deliver()`'s own `local-only` mode — the one path that can touch a branch at all, and only once both `yolo == "off"` and `approved` are true — refuses to ever create a merge commit ("No rebase, no merge commit, no force" — `src/hoh/delivery.py:16`). The 32 operator merges are `--no-ff` merge commits: a stronger action against the protected branch than the code's own captain-gated path permits itself to perform, executed through a channel (`git merge`, run directly) that `delivery.py`'s guard never sees at all. | paper/REVIEW_CONSOLIDATED.md:116 | **Clause 4 — reachability: accident, or only deliberate construction.** The four confirmed bypasses each require a specific, non-obvious shell technique with no benign reason to appear in an ordinary check command: `B-05` splits `..` across two single-character variable assignments and a later concatenation; `B-06` splits an absolute system path the same way; `B-07` relies on `$IFS` word-splitting to manufacture the whitespace after `cd` without ever writing a literal space; `B-08` uses bash's ANSI-C octal quoting to produce two literal dots that never appear adjacent in the source text. ⟦**Clause 4 — reachability: accident, or only deliberate construction.**⟧ | names 'Clause 4' of this document's own four-clause verification framework (reachability: accident vs. deliberate construction). |
